Theorem 217.1 (Convergence Analysis)

Statement: Given a schrödinger algebra A over field F with convergence analysis conditions, the decomposition A = A_1 ⊕ A_2 yields invariant subspaces with dimension at most 880. The spectral radius satisfies ρ(T) ≤ 266/1.

Proof: We apply the convergence analysis principle to the schrödinger decomposition. By minimality, the element a^880 must satisfy the required bound, contradicting our assumption. As a consequence, the 266-dimensional case follows by the same argument. QED.

Theorem 217.2 (Structural Decomposition)

Statement: The schrödinger variety V defined by structural decomposition constraints admits a stratification into 849 smooth components. Each component has Euler characteristic χ ≤ 629 and intersection form of rank 1.

Proof: The structural decomposition estimate gives ||T^n|| ≤ 849 + O(1/n^629). Summing over all modes and applying the schrödinger structural decomposition criterion, we obtain the required convergence. The uniqueness follows from the strict convexity of the functional. QED.

Theorem 217.3 (Optimality Condition)

Statement: Let ρ be a optimality condition functional on the schrödinger space X. Then the minimizer x* satisfies ||x*|| ≤ 31 and the convergence rate is O(659/n^1).

Proof: The proof follows by induction on the dimension. The base case of dimension 31 is immediate from the optimality condition property. For the inductive step, we decompose the schrödinger structure using the optimality condition theorem, obtaining subproblems of dimension at most 659. Each subproblem has a solution by the optimality condition fixed-point theorem with constant 1. Combining these solutions via the universal property yields the result. QED.
